# Mathlib comment- and docstring-consistency audit

_Generated by `scripts/comment_audit/comment_audit.py` on 2026-10-08; 126472 comments in 8724 files scanned._

The companion of [`naming_audit.md`](naming_audit.md), which audits declarations:
this document audits the prose around them — module docstrings, declaration
docstrings and ordinary comments.

This is a living document. Every row is identified by its category, token and file
(not the line number). Re-running the script keeps the `Status` and `Note` cells of rows that are
still present, adds new rows as `open`, and drops rows that no longer apply. Triage by editing
the `Status`/`Note` cells in place.

Statuses: `open` (not yet looked at), `confirmed` (checked by hand, should be fixed), `fp`
(false positive or accepted exception), `fixed` (corrected; the row disappears on the next run),
`wontfix` (real but deliberately left alone).

Confidence (`conf`) is the scanner's own estimate of how likely a row is a genuine problem; it is
not a triage verdict.

## Scope

- **Not** plain misspellings: those are category
  [F1](naming_audit.md#f1-probable-spelling-errors-in-comments-and-docstrings) of the naming
  audit, which spell-checks the corpus against itself. This document covers the ways in which
  comments disagree with *each other*.
- Every threshold that could be a matter of taste is derived from the corpus instead: a
  spelling, heading or marker is reported because Mathlib overwhelmingly writes it another
  way, not because a style guide or a dictionary prefers one form. The counts behind each
  verdict are in the `Detail` column, so a row can be argued with.
- Checks that a linter already enforces (`lake exe lint-style`, the `docString`, `docPrime`
  and `header` linters: empty docstrings, a docstring ending in a comma, trailing
  whitespace, the copyright header, Verso syntax) are left to the linter and not repeated.

## Conventions applied

- A module docstring is `/-! # Title … -/`, subdivided by `##` sections whose spellings are
  fixed: `Main definitions`, `Main statements`/`Main results`, `Notation`,
  `Implementation notes`, `References`, `Tags`, `TODO`.
- A cross-reference resolves: a file path names a file, `Mathlib.Foo.Bar` names a module or a
  declaration, `note [foo]` names a `library_note «foo»`, and `[authorYEAR]` names an entry
  of `docs/references.bib`.
- Comments are English prose in Mathlib's own spelling, and mathematics inside them is
  written in Unicode (`ℝ`, `→`, `⊆`), not in ASCII or LaTeX.
- A word is spelled the same way throughout: hyphenation and British/American endings are
  not chosen per file.
- `TODO`, `Porting note`, `Adaptation note` and `See also` are written one way.

## Findings

### A1: Section headings that differ only in letter case

Module docstrings are divided by `##` headings. Mathlib writes `## Main definitions`, `## Implementation notes`, `## References`, `## Tags`; a heading that differs from the dominant spelling only in capitalisation (`## Main Definitions`, `## Implementation Notes`) is a pure inconsistency. The dominant spelling is taken from the corpus, not from a fixed list, and the ratio of the two counts sets the confidence.

375 row(s).

### A2: Section headings with trailing punctuation

A `##` heading is a title, not a sentence: `## Main definitions:` and `## References:` render the colon as part of the heading. The overwhelming majority of Mathlib's headings carry no trailing punctuation.

116 row(s).

### A3: Rare variants of a standard section heading

A heading that is a singular/plural variant of a common one (`## Main definition`, `## Reference`), or a synonym that Mathlib's documentation style replaces by another heading (`## Keywords` → `## Tags`, `## Implementation details` → `## Implementation notes`). Headings that are genuinely specific to one file (`## Proof outline`, `## Mathematical background`) are not variants of anything and are not reported.

### A4: Heading structure anomalies in a module docstring

A module docstring opens with a single `#` title and subdivides it with `##`. Reported here: a header docstring with no `#` title, one with more than one `#` title (two documents in one file), and a `###` subsection that is not inside any `##` section.

106 row(s).

| Status | Anomaly | Location | Detail | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| confirmed | `several `#` titles` | `Mathlib/RingTheory/AdicCompletion/LocalRing.lean:25` | (high) 2 `#` titles in one module docstring: `Basic Properties of Complete Local Ring`, `Main Results` | fix: `## Main results`. Change `# Main Results` to `## Main results` on line 25: a second `#` title in one module docstring, whereas it is a section of the document titled by the first `#`. Also sentence-cases `Results` (the DOC-A1 convention, which applies once the heading is a `##`). |
| confirmed | `several `#` titles` | `Mathlib/RingTheory/Flat/IsBaseChange.lean:19` | (high) 2 `#` titles in one module docstring: `Lemmas about `IsBaseChange` under exact sequences`, `Main Results` | fix: `## Main results`. Change `# Main Results` to `## Main results` on line 21: a second `#` title in one module docstring, whereas it is a section of the document titled by the first `#`. Also sentence-cases `Results` (the DOC-A1 convention, which applies once the heading is a `##`). |
| confirmed | `several `#` titles` | `Mathlib/RingTheory/Kaehler/JacobiZariski.lean:43` | (high) 2 `#` titles in one module docstring: `The Jacobi-Zariski exact sequence`, `TODO` | fix: `## TODO`. ### A5: Citations with no entry in `docs/references.bib`

A `## References` section cites sources by bibliography key, as `* [Author, *Title*][authorYEAR]`. A key with no entry in `docs/references.bib` produces a dangling link in the generated documentation. Only citation-shaped keys (a name followed by a year, or a hyphenated name) are checked, so that `[R]` and `[simp]` are not mistaken for citations.

### B1: References to source files that do not exist

### B3: References to library notes that are not defined

### C1: Minority spelling of a word Mathlib writes one way

Hyphenation (`non-zero` vs `nonzero`) and British/American spelling (`normalised` vs `normalized`, `neighbourhood` vs `neighborhood`) counted over the whole comment corpus: a form is reported only when the other one is at least five times as common and used at least ten times. One row per word and file. Mathlib has no rule on either axis, so these rows document an inconsistency rather than an error; a hyphenated word whose closed form is a different word (`re-cover` vs `recover`) is an `fp`.

### C2: Proper nouns written in lower case

A word that Mathlib's comments capitalise at least five times as often as not (`Noetherian`, `Hausdorff`, `Lipschitz`, `Boolean`), written in lower case outside a code span. Adjectives that Mathlib deliberately keeps lower case (`abelian`) are dominated by the lower-case form and are therefore never reported.

### C3: Doubled words

The same word twice in a row in prose (`the the`, `that that`). Code spans, fenced blocks and lines that look like ASCII diagrams are excluded. A few rows are correct English (`at at most one point`, `two two-pointings`) and are `fp`.

### D1: ASCII or LaTeX stand-ins for Mathlib's notation

Mathlib writes mathematics in Unicode. An ASCII `->`, `<->`, `<=`, `>=`, `!=` or a LaTeX `\mathbb{R}`, `\to`, `\forall` that is neither inside a code span nor inside `$…$` maths is a stand-in that escaped, and renders literally in the generated documentation. Every LaTeX command Mathlib's comments contain is currently inside maths mode, so only the ASCII rows remain; the LaTeX patterns are kept as a guard. `->` is `low` throughout: it is also how a rename note (`succ n -> n + 1`) and an ASCII diagram are written, both of which are `fp`.

### D2: Unbalanced code-span delimiters

A docstring with an odd number of backticks: one code span is never closed, so the rest of the docstring renders as code (or the following prose is swallowed). Fenced blocks and ``double spans`` are accounted for first.

### D4: `--` comments without a space after the dashes

Mathlib writes `-- comment`, not `--comment`. Separator lines (`---`), `--!` and `--#` are not reported.
